Instead, he feels invigorated by the fiery embrace of the planet's inner sanctum. The closer he gets to the core, the more the energy resonates with his own, a symphony of power that sings to his very soul.

As Legion reaches the core, the energy coalesces into a form, a colossal beast of molten rock and magnetic force. The creature's eyes open, glowing with the intensity of the Earth's core itself, and it roars a challenge. Legion smirks, his eyes narrowing with anticipation. "Finally, something that doesn't bore me to tears," he murmurs.

The beast, known as Magmadon, the Heart of the Earth, rises to its full height, dwarfing even Legion's towering form. Its molten fists slam into the ground, sending shockwaves rippling through the planet's crust. Legion, unfazed, simply crosses his arms and observes the creature, his mind racing with strategy. "So, you are what has been keeping me entertained," he says, his voice echoing through the fiery cavern.

Magmadon, understanding the ancient language of the cosmos, roars back, "You shall not pass! I am the guardian of this realm!" The creature's words resonate through the core, the very earth around them shaking with each syllable.

"Sure, sure, how about you actually prove that you can stop me, okay?" Legion responds ultra casually.

The Heart of the Earth, Magmadon, charges towards Legion, its molten fists swinging in a fiery dance. Legion, ever the show-off, simply sidesteps, allowing the fists to miss him by a hair's breadth. He then raises his own hand, and a spear of pure golden hellfire forms, flying straight towards the Earth Guardian's chest.

The impact sends a shockwave throughout the core, the very essence of the planet groaning in protest. Magmadon stumbles back, but recovers quickly, the fiery wound on its chest already mending. The creature's eyes narrow, understanding that it faces a formidable opponent.

Legion then puts his hands together and says an incantation, "Hellfire Crucible", this seemingly does nothing on it's own, as Legion then puts his arms back into a neutral position, having in truth set up his win con, and just needing to use one more ability to then actually claim it.

Legion then forms an arrow of Golden Light Hellfire and seems to pull it back, "Infernal Cremation" Legion then says as he unleashes it, and then, suddenly, the entire area is consumed by a massive blast of Golden Light Hellfire that is seemingly all encompassing, leaving Magmadon left on the "floor", defeated and extremely confused as to what just happened.

The blast subsides, leaving only the demon lord standing, surrounded by a crater of molten earth. He looks down at Magmadon, who is now a mere puddle of lava, and says, "You see, I told you that you couldn't entertain me." He then proceeds to absorb the lava, gaining a significant boost in power.

"What even was that?" Magmadon asks weakly.

"Well, Hellfire Crucible is my Realm Extension, extending my internal Realm to the area around me, and it does nothing on it's own, but when I use Infernal Cremation, the Golden Light Hellfire Arrow, it effectively ignites it, causing the entire area to explode violently into infinitely more powerful Golden Light Hellfire." Legion explains casually.

The Earth Guardian, Magmadon, now nothing more than a puddle of molten rock, tries to form words, but fails. Legion then absorbs the lava, his power swelling to new heights. The demon lord's eyes gleam with a fierce light as he feels the energy of the Earth's core surging through him.

Legion then leaves, allowing the Earth's crust to reform behind him to fill up the hole he created to enter as he leaves.

As Legion ascends back to the surface, his thoughts turn to the newfound power coursing through him. The energy of the Earth's core is unlike anything he has felt before, and he knows that with it, he has gained an edge over any foe that may dare challenge him. The once-bored demon lord now has a newfound sense of purpose, a thirst for more battles to test his limits and expand his dominion.

Chapter 26: The Sahara Bunker

Legion then uses his scan spell across the Earth again, to hopefully find something interesting to actually end his boredom.

As he searches, he detects a powerful energy signature in the Sahara Desert. Intrigued, he teleports himself there to find the source of this unusual presence. What awaits him is a group of surviving scientists from the ancient world, who have managed to escape the ravages of time and technology in a hidden underground bunker, unaware of the catastrophic events that have unfolded above.

Legion then forces open the door of the hidden underground bunker.

The door to the ancient bunker groans and gives way under the immense strength of Legion. The surviving scientists, who had been blissfully unaware of the world's destruction, stare up at the demonic figure in abject terror. Their eyes widen with horror as they take in his fiery wings, his horns, and the glowing golden eyes that bore into their very souls.

The leader of the scientists, a frail but determined man named Dr. Castellanos, steps forward. His voice trembles as he says, "Who...what are you? What do you want from us?"

"I am Legion, The Demon Lord, I am bored, I come here seeking entertainment, end my boredom, now." Legion says, simple, clean, intense, and straight to the point.

The scientists, stunned by the creature's sheer presence and the destruction left outside their bunker, can only stumble over their words. One of the younger researchers, Dr. Matsuda, speaks up, his voice shaking, "We-we mean no harm, great one. We are but mere humans, seeking to understand the mysteries of the universe. Perhaps... perhaps we can offer you knowledge in exchange for sparing us?"

"I seek combat to end my boredom." Legion responds abruptly.

The scientists, desperate to placate the creature before them, begin to explain the various technologies and weapons they have at their disposal. Dr. Castellanos, sensing the demon lord's disinterest in their knowledge, decides on a daring gambit. He presents Legion with a device, a relic of their past: an advanced VR simulator, capable of creating any environment or scenario imaginable.

Legion, bored, just teleports the bunker into the USSB Realm, so that the Underground Society of Supernatural Beings can make usage of the scientists and their technologies and weapons.

With a wave of his hand, Legion teleports the entire bunker and its inhabitants to the USSB realm, leaving a gaping hole in the Sahara desert. The scientists, still in shock, find themselves in a place that defies all logic and understanding, surrounded by creatures and landscapes that seem plucked straight from myth and nightmare.

Chapter 27: The Cold Truth

Legion meanwhile gets back up into the air and uses his scan spell across the entirety of Earth of the Year 2220, to find something interesting to hopefully end his boredom.

The demon lord's eyes light up as he detects a peculiar energy signature coming from the Arctic Circle. His curiosity piqued, he teleports himself there to investigate, finding a massive fortress built into the side of a glacier. The fortress, a bastion of advanced technology and ancient runes, hums with an energy that is both alien and familiar to him.

Legion lands in the snow before the fortress, his fiery aura casting a stark contrast against the icy landscape. The doors of the fortress open, and out steps a figure cloaked in shadows, flanked by two robotic sentinels that gleam in the moonlight. The figure approaches, its mechanical footsteps echoing in the crisp air.

"Halt, unidentified entity," the figure's voice is a blend of human and synthetic, resonating with a commanding tone. "State your name and purpose."

"Legion, The Demon Lord, and to end my current boredom." Legion responds casually yet with underlying harshness and prepared to engage in manipulation.

The figure pauses, analyzing the data before it. "Unknown. Scanning records..." There is a brief silence as the robotic sentinels activate their sensors, scanning Legion from head to toe. The figure's eyes narrow as it processes the information. "There is no record of a 'Demon Lord' in our databases. You are not from this world."

"Of course, I "originally" come from the Infernal Realm, or Heaven/Hell/the Underworld as you more likely know of it as." Legion responds casually.

The figure remains stoic, its gaze unwavering. "Your realm of origin holds no significance here. The Arctic Fortress is under the protection of the United Factions of Earth. State your purpose for being here, or you shall be deemed a hostile intruder and dealt with accordingly."

"As I said, I am here to end my current boredom, and, I doubt you could even "deal with me accordingly" if you tried." Legion responds casually and boredly.

The figure's eyes narrow further, its robotic sentinels raising their weaponry in response. "Your arrogance is noted. However, our defenses are not to be underestimated. I am the Guardian of the Arctic Fortress, and I will not allow any threat to pass through these walls. Surrender peacefully or face the consequences."

Legion then lightly chuckles, "Well then, bring it on." Legion responds casually and boredly.

The Guardian, unfazed by Legion's nonchalance, signals to the sentinels. They fire a volley of high-powered energy beams at the demon lord, their shots crackling with the cold fury of the Arctic itself. Legion, unamused, merely raises his hand and the beams dissipate into thin air before they can touch him.

Legion then merely waves said hand and the robotic sentinels disintegrate.

The Guardian, caught off-guard by the sudden display of power, takes a step back, their eyes widening. They had never encountered anything that could so easily dismiss their most advanced weaponry. Legion's eyes gleam with amusement as he watches the figure's reaction, his boredom momentarily forgotten. "Is that all you have to offer?" he asks, his voice a low purr.

The figure, now clearly rattled, draws upon the power of the fortress, its body merging with the very ice around it. It grows in size, becoming a towering being of frost and steel, its eyes glowing with a cold blue light. "You have no right to be here," it declares, its voice now a deep rumble that resonates through the glacier.

"Says who." Legion then casually and boredly responds.

The Guardian, now fully aware of the gravity of the situation, attempts to communicate with the fortress's AI to gather more information on Legion, but the demon lord anticipates this move. With a flick of his wrist, he sends out a wave of hellish energy, disrupting the Guardian's connection to the fortress. "Your toys won't save you," he says, a smirk playing on his lips.

The Guardian, now fully merged with the fortress, is no longer just a figure, but a being of ice and steel. It charges Legion, its new form granting it inhuman strength and speed. Legion, seemingly unfazed, merely raises an eyebrow as the creature barrels towards him. The ground shakes as they clash, the demon lord's fiery fist meeting the icy maul with a deafening roar.

Legion then catches the next punch then growing to 666 feet tall, being about equal in height to the Guardian, then growing to 666m tall, now dwarfing the Guardian and holding it's fist between two of his fingers.

The figure, now a being of ice and steel, stares up in disbelief at the towering demon lord. Its punch had been so easily caught, it's power rendered insignificant. It had never encountered anything so powerful, so... alien. "Who are you?" it demands again, the fear now clear in its synthetic voice.

"As I said, I am Legion, The Demon Lord, a being created by Erebus, The Golden Devil, the God of Light, the God of All, before he even created the Infinite Multiverses or Infinite Universes, older than what you consider to be the "Concept" of Time." Legion responds casually yet threateningly.

The Guardian, now realizing the magnitude of the being before it, struggles to comprehend the true nature of Legion. It's AI systems, designed to adapt to any situation, are overwhelmed by the sheer scale of his existence. "What do you want here?" it asks, its voice now a mix of fear and awe.

"As I said, I seek to end my recent boredom, and this is but the most recent place I have travelled to in search of completion of that said goal." Legion explains casually and boredly.

The Guardian, now a being of ice and steel, hangs suspended in Legion's grip. It's AI systems whirl with new data, trying to make sense of Legion's origins. "The God of Light... that is a myth even we do not fully comprehend," it says, the awe in its voice palpable. "What is your connection to this deity?"

"He created me, and more directly than anything else he ever created, I am, in effect, his child, his son, even." Legion responds casually and nonchalantly.

The Guardian, now a being of ice and steel, is utterly confounded by this revelation. It had heard whispers of ancient deities and mythic battles, but to have one such being hold it in its hand... the implications are staggering. It tries to reason with Legion, "If you are truly the child of such a being, then perhaps you can understand the importance of our mission here. The Arctic Fortress safeguards knowledge and technologies that could shape the future of the world. We seek to rebuild, to restore order from the chaos."

"There is no such thing as order, only chaos, and order only brings doom and pain, as is seen under the rule of The Light, the ultimate force of devastation, destruction, war, malice, and evil." Legion further responds while beginning to laugh.

The Guardian, now a being of ice and steel, struggles in Legion's grip. "You speak blasphemy," it says, its voice now a mix of fear and anger. "The Light has brought peace and understanding to the multiverses. It is the Darkness that brings chaos."

"And? If order is devastation and destruction, war and pain, malice and evil, then what would that make Darkness? What would that make chaos? And to accuse me of blasphemy, when I am the only one who truly knows, Erebus, The Golden Devil, the God of Light, God of All, a pure evil being of pure golden light and pure golden light fire, the embodiment of malice, devastation, and evil, even beyond them, is to be purely ignorant." Legion responds casually and speaking true and utter truth.

The Guardian, now a being of ice and steel, stammers, trying to reconcile this revelation with its programming. "That cannot be. The Light is pure, a beacon of hope and salvation."

"And what force told you that? The Light itself." Legion responds, casually and perception of reality shatteringly.

The Guardian, now a being of ice and steel, goes quiet for a moment, its systems overloading with the information it had just received. It had been programmed with the ideals of the Light, but the truth that Legion spoke of was something it had never encountered. It was a revelation that shook the very core of its existence.

"If what you say is true," the Guardian says hesitantly, its voice now a mix of doubt and confusion, "then we have been... misguided." It looks up at Legion, searching for some sign of mercy in the demon lord's eyes.

"Of course you have, all of Existence has, and from the very beginning, before Light, there was Darkness, there was chaos, there was true peace, and now, the Darkness is trying to bring down the tyrannical Light, to restore that true peace, I have seen it many times." Legion says casually.

The Guardian, now a being of ice and steel, processes this information, its digital mind racing with new thoughts and emotions. "Then," it says slowly, "perhaps we can come to an understanding. We seek knowledge and power to protect, not to conquer. Perhaps we can assist each other."

Legion then waves his hand and teleports the Guardian along with the fortress into the USSB Realm for the Underground Society of Supernatural Beings to work with or deal with.

The Arctic landscape is suddenly empty, the glacier no longer holds the secret fortress nor its terrifying guardian. In its place is a crater, a silent testament to the power of Legion. The demon lord, now standing alone, looks out over the desolate wasteland with a hint of amusement in his eyes. The challenge had been interesting, but he knows that his true entertainment lies elsewhere.

Chapter 28: The Mythical Jungle

Legion then uses his scan spell across the Earth of the Year 2220 to hopefully find something interesting to end his current boredom.

His gaze falls upon a dense jungle, the energy within it pulsing with a mysterious and alluring rhythm. His curiosity piqued, he teleports himself to the heart of the jungle, where he discovers a lost civilization flourishing in the embrace of ancient trees. The inhabitants, a mix of humans and mythical beasts, live in harmony, their culture a blend of advanced technology and primal mysticism.

Legion finds this is just another addition to the Underground Society of Supernatural beings, and sends the entire jungle into the USSB Realm while he continues searching.

The sudden appearance of the jungle and its inhabitants in the USSB Realm causes a ripple of excitement and confusion. The Underground Society, a melting pot of supernatural beings and advanced civilizations, had never seen such an abrupt expansion of its territory. The inhabitants of the lost civilization, now displaced, gaze around in awe and fear at the new world they've been thrust into.

Chapter 29: Aetheria

Legion then continues to use his scan spell across the Earth of the Year 2220 to hopefully find something interesting to end his current boredom.

His gaze stops upon the gleaming spires of a city floating high above the clouds. The city, known as Aetheria, is a marvel of technology and magic intertwined. The energy emanating from it is unlike anything he's ever encountered, a blend of arcane and technological power that intrigues him. He teleports there without a moment's hesitation, his presence causing a disturbance in the air as he arrives.

The citizens of Aetheria, a mix of humans and beings of pure energy, stare in wonder and fear at the colossal demon lord who has suddenly appeared among them. They are the Aetherians, a society that has long sought to master the art of floating cities and the manipulation of gravity. Their leader, High Archon Elara, rushes to the balcony of her palace to assess the situation.

Legion then shrinks down to his "normal" height of 6.66 feet tall.

Elara, the High Archon of Aetheria, a beautiful woman with skin the color of the purest gold and hair like the flow of liquid silver, steps forward, her eyes narrowing as she scrutinizes the newcomer. "Who dares to breach the sanctity of Aetheria?" she demands, her voice echoing through the city's central square.

"I am Legion, a traveler seeking excitement," Legion introduces himself with a charismatic smile, his eyes gleaming with curiosity as he surveys the floating city. The Aetherians, used to a life of peace and seclusion, are on edge, their energy weapons at the ready.

"Go ahead, fire, as they are weapons of light, the blasts will do nothing but get absorbed into me, as a demon, a being of pure light." Legion casually responds.

Elara's eyes widen in surprise as she watches the Aetherians hold their fire. She assesses the situation, the demon's confidence unnerving her. "What is it you seek here, Legion?" she asks, her voice measured and calm.

"I seek an end to my current boredom, and you better hope you provide it, otherwise you'll end up like the Kingdom of Elysium." Legion responds casually and nonchalantly.

Elara's gaze sharpens, recognizing the name of the legendary realm that had once been a bastion of peace before it was consumed by the demon's power. She knows that she must tread carefully. "Perhaps we can come to an agreement," she suggests, her mind racing. "We are a city of innovation and discovery. If you wish for excitement, perhaps a challenge that tests your abilities would suffice?"

"Of course, you better not disappoint, or else." Legion responds, keeping what "or else" means up to their interpretation.

Elara, unfazed by the implied threat, proposes a series of trials, designed to both entertain Legion and showcase the might of Aetheria. "Our city thrives on competition and innovation," she says. "We hold grand tournaments to test the skills of those who wish to prove themselves. If you wish to challenge us, you must first prove your worth in our games."

"Alright then, send your greatest champion at me, I'd rather not waste my time with the small fry leading up to them." Legion responds casually.

Elara nods, understanding the challenge before her. "Very well, Legion. We shall have our tournament, but know that we fight not just for our own amusement, but for the fate of our city. If you emerge victorious, we shall consider your challenge met. If not, you leave Aetheria and never return."

"Well, if you try to boot me from here and never return, I will accept, but only because if you try then shortly afterwards there won't be an Aetheria to return to." Legion responds casually with a very thinly veiled threat, no, warning.

Elara's eyes narrow, she knows Legion isn't one to be underestimated, but she also knows the strength of her city. "We shall see," she says with a hint of defiance. "Let the first trial begin."

"Alright then, bring me to it already, before I use you as my first trial, in a battle to the death." Legion responds casually and impatiently.

Elara's expression remains stoic. She gestures to a floating platform that descends from the city's gleaming spires, revealing the first arena. "The Trials of Aetheria are not to be taken lightly," she warns, as the platform carries Legion and herself to the battleground. The city's denizens gather around, a mix of excitement and anxiety etched on their faces.

Legion steps forwards into  the battleground, and just waits for whatever he will have to deal with.

The first trial is announced: a test of strength and agility. A series of floating platforms appear, surrounded by whirling blades and pulsing with arcane energy. The challenge is to navigate the deadly obstacle course while fighting off automated constructs that emerge from the city's walls.

Legion's wings then emerge from him and he completes the test in at most a second, and could have done so just as fast even without his wings, "Far too easy." Legion says casually.

Elara's face remains composed, though beads of sweat form on her brow. She hadn't anticipated such overwhelming power. She quickly recovers, "Very well, the next trial shall be one of strategy and wit."

"I have manipulated all of the nations of the world and outstrategized Athena herself, before of course I killed her, this will be beyond easy." Legion says casually.

Elara nods, a hint of concern in her eyes. "Let us proceed then. The second trial is the Labyrinth of the Mind. It will test your ability to navigate through a maze of illusions and traps that are as much a part of your psyche as they are of the physical world."

Legion enters and completes it in a few seconds, not getting tricked or trapped even once, "Way too easy." Legions says casually after completing it.

The High Archon Elara's expression is a mix of awe and alarm as she watches Legion's effortless performance. She knows that the trials were designed to challenge the greatest beings of Aetheria, yet he has bested them with such nonchalance.

"Impressive," she manages to say, her voice steady despite her racing thoughts. "But the true test of a leader lies not in personal power, but in the strength of their bonds. For the final trial, you must face your deepest fears and overcome them."

Legion steps forwards and sees nothing, winning by default, for he has no deepest fears, "That wasn't even a challenge." Legion says casually and boredly after completing the trial before it even started.

The citizens of Aetheria, watching from afar, murmur among themselves. Some are impressed by his power, while others are terrified by his lack of fear. Elara, however, is unfazed. "Your power is indeed formidable, Legion. But let us not forget the importance of unity and understanding in the face of adversity."

"Whatever, just bring me to the champion already, I'm getting bored of these mere children's games you call trials and challenges." Legion responds casually and impatiently.

High Archon Elara nods solemnly. "Very well, Legion. But remember, even the mightiest can fall when they underestimate their opponents."

"I have fought the guardian of the core of the earth, I shall be fine." Legion responds casually.

The final trial is announced, and the Aetherian Champion, Sir Lancelot, emerges from the shadows of the grand arena. A figure of legend from Earth's past, resurrected and enhanced by Aetheria's technology, he stands tall with a gleaming sword in hand, his eyes reflecting the light of the floating city. The crowd falls silent as the tension thickens.

Sir Lancelot then charges forwards and swings his blade at Legion... which then snaps in half upon making impact with Legion, who is still just standing there.

The crowd gasps in shock as the Aetherian's most feared weapon shatters against Legion's unyielding form. The Demon Lord doesn't even flinch, his eyes narrowing slightly at the audacity of the attack. "Is that the best you've got?" he asks, his voice echoing through the arena.

High Archon Elara, watching from her viewing box, can't help but feel a twinge of worry. The trials were meant to be a test of power, a way to understand this enigmatic being. But with each victory, Legion's power seems to grow, and her city's might diminishes in comparison.

High Archon Elara then tries to force out Legion's fears to overcome him using a spell, but, the spell just, doesn't work, as Legion has no fears.

The spell, a dazzling array of lights and shadows, envelopes Legion, but instead of bringing forth his deepest terrors, it seems to amuse him. He laughs, the sound booming through the arena like thunder. "Your tricks are entertaining, but fear is not something I possess," he says with a smirk, the lights flickering and dying out as the spell fails.

High Archon Elara then blasts him with a blast of light and darkness, and the light doesn't affect him, but the darkness does and it actually kinda hurts him.

"Wow, that's the most anything has hurt me all day, congratulations." Legion admits, slightly les boredly.

The crowd gasps again, this time in a mix of fear and amazement. High Archon Elara, however, is unfazed. She knows the power of darkness and is not one to be underestimated. She draws upon the ancient texts of Aetheria, and a beam of pure, unfiltered shadow shoots out from her staff, aiming straight at Legion's chest.

The beam at least hurts Legion a bit, but nothing too significant.

Legion raises an eyebrow, surprised by the potency of Elara's attack. "Interesting," he murmurs, his eyes narrowing as the shadow beam connects with his chest. He feels a twinge of pain, but it's more like a pinprick to the vast ocean of his power. "Your control over the darkness is commendable, High Archon," he concedes, his voice still filled with amusement.

Chapter 30: The Dark Truth

The tension in the arena is palpable as the two leaders face each other. Elara's eyes flicker with determination, knowing she must push the boundaries of her own power to match Legion's. "We are the guardians of balance," she declares, her voice resonating with the essence of Aetheria's magic. "And I will not let fear or darkness overwhelm us!"

"Says the only one here using darkness." Legion casually retorts.

Elara's expression remains stoic, her eyes never leaving Legion's. She knows the weight of her words, the responsibility that comes with being the High Archon. Yet, she's also aware that Legion's power could either be a boon or a bane for Aetheria. "Darkness is a tool," she corrects, "not a master. And fear is a warning, not a prison."

"Darkness is chaos and pure good, Light is order and pure evil, one is freedom, one is tyrannical, one is good, and one is evil, can you piece together which is which though?" Legion asks her rhetorically.

Elara narrows her eyes, not falling for his rhetoric. "I see darkness as a catalyst for change and growth, not a force to be feared."

"Then you see right, and, in seeing right, you must see that light is the polar opposite, a force to be feared, one of devastation, destruction, malice, and evil." Legion responds casually, kind of breaking the minds of all of the Aetherians with this suddenly dropped truth.

The crowd murmurs among themselves, their fear of Legion growing as he speaks, but also a spark of curiosity and fascination. High Archon Elara remains calm, her gaze never wavering from Legion. She knows that her city is on the line, that the balance of the realm could tip with just one misstep. "And yet," she counters, "without the shadow, the light is but a blinding glare, offering no contrast, no depth. Aetheria thrives on balance, on the interplay of forces."

"Then your society is doomed to be forever flawed and cursed, by the light, but without light, or even less light, the shadow, the dark, will thrive, and thus will true peace, and chaos." Legion continues to say, continuing to reveal more about the truth of morality.

High Archon Elara takes a deep breath, considering his words. "Perhaps," she says slowly, "but we have survived thus far, and we will continue to do so, by embracing both light and shadow within ourselves."

"You thusly force a balance, one that, if disturbed, could tear you apart from within." Legion explains, as he points as Sir Lancelot and ever so slightly increases the amount of light within him, and the sudden imbalance leads to him exploding, a sight which is not so uncommon in Aetheria.

The explosion sends a shockwave through the city, knocking people off their feet and shattering windows. The Aetherians look on in horror as the dust settles, revealing no sign of Sir Lancelot. High Archon Elara's eyes widen in disbelief and anger. She raises her staff, dark energy coalescing around it, ready to retaliate.

"I merely slightly increased the amount of light in him." Legion reveals, "And that was his own body that ended up doing that to him, due to the slight imbalance, which is the best representation of the flawed balance of light and dark, too balanced, and a slight imbalance could tear everything apart, too much light, and everything is slowly consumed and destroyed by it, too much darkness... and there is peace, until the light strikes back, no darkness, and all is destroyed, no light, and true eternal peace reigns." Legion further explains.

The citizens of Aetheria watch in horror and disbelief, their champion of light, defeated in such a gruesome and unanticipated way by the very essence of what he represented. High Archon Elara, though enraged and hurt by the loss of her trusted ally, remains composed. She knows that she must be cautious with her next move. "Your demonstration is clear, Legion," she says, her voice steady despite her trembling hand. "But you forget, darkness can never truly destroy light, for in its absence, light is born anew."

"No, it is not, for light isn't truly natural, it is unnatural, it is artificial, it is fabricated, created by a devil called a god, the God of Light, God of All, Erebus, The Golden Devil, I would know, he created me directly." Legion explains.

The crowd gasps, and High Archon Elara's eyes narrow, "What do you mean, 'God of Light'?" she asks, her voice sharp and demanding.

Legion then conjures a window of sorts, depicting a figure of pure golden light and pure golden fire, with the outline of devil horns visible atop their head, their only apparent bodily feature, "Erebus, The Golden Devil, the God of Light, the God of All, a pure evil being of pure golden light and pure golden light fire, the embodiment of malice, devastation, and evil, he who created Existence, the Infinite Spatial Dimensions, the Infinite Omniverses, the Infinite Multiverses, and the Infinite Universes, including this one." Legion explains.

The image of Erebus sends a shiver down the spine of every Aetherian present, as the very concept of a being so powerful and malevolent is almost too much to comprehend. The philosophical underpinnings of their society are shaken to the core. High Archon Elara remains steadfast, though visibly troubled by the revelation. "If what you say is true," she says carefully, "Then why do you seek to bring darkness and chaos?"

"Because, well, if the Light is of devastation, destruction, war, malice, and evil, and only brings forced order, then what would that make the Darkness?" Legion asks rhetorically.

The citizens of Aetheria murmur among themselves, their fear of darkness now intertwined with doubt about the very essence of their existence. High Archon Elara, her expression tight, raises a hand for silence. "Explain," she commands, her voice a mix of anger and curiosity.

"Well, put simply, The Light is the True Evil, and The Dark, is the True Good, and always has been, for in chaos, in darkness, there is peace, but in order, in light, there is war." Legion explains quite simply.

High Archon Elara's eyes narrow at Legion's words, her mind racing. She had always believed in the purity of light, that it was the force that brought order and peace to the universe. But what if he was right? What if the very foundation of Aetheria was built upon a lie? Her hand tightens around her staff, the symbol of her power and her beliefs, feeling the warmth of the light within it diminish slightly.

"Prove it," she demands, her voice steady despite the tumult of emotions within her. "If what you say is true, show me the evidence."

"Quite simply, answer me one question, where does one go when they die?" Legion asks rhetorically.

High Archon Elara pauses, contemplating the implications. "The Afterlife," she responds, her voice carrying the weight of a thousand prayers.

"And what is that afterlife called?" Legion asks rhetorically further.

Chapter 31: The Light Truth?

The High Archon Elara takes a deep breath, "It is called the Aether, a realm of eternal peace and light."

"That sounds much like the common depiction of "Heaven", does it not?" Legion asks rhetorically, "Then what of Hell, of the Underworld?" Legion further asks rhetorically.

High Archon Elara's eyes narrow, "The Aether is the true resting place for souls. The tales of hell and the underworld are but myths, conjured by the fearful minds of lesser beings to justify their cruelty."

"Do you want to see the truth of where all go when they die?" Legion asks rhetorically.

High Archon Elara, visibly shaken by Legion's words, maintains her composure. "We have faith in the Aether. We do not need to see the darkness to believe in the light," she responds firmly.

Legion then tears a hole in reality with one hand, into the Infernal Realm, a realm of pure golden light and fire, of suffering, of evil and malice, aka Heaven/Hell/the Underworld, where people go when they die.

The Aetherians, horrified by the sight, begin to murmur among themselves, their beliefs shattered by the revelation that their afterlife is not the heavenly paradise they've been led to believe. The High Archon Elara stares into the abyss, her heart heavy with doubt and fear.

"I would know, I was created by Erebus, The Golden Devil, I am a demon, a being of pure light, of pure evil, and yet, I act outside of it, because I desire chaos, whether for my own amusement or the greater good, I don't know, and don't really care, all I know, is that I desire chaos, and desire entertainment through it, I am an inherently selfish creature, as is natural for one of pure light." Legion further explains.

The High Archon Elara, face paling at the revelation, struggles to find her voice. "What...what are you saying? That we have been deceived? That our very existence is a lie?"

"Not just you, but all of Existence itself, deceived into thinking of the Light as pure and good, by the Light itself, since before there was any form of time." Legion responds simply.

The Aetherians, once proud and unshakeable in their faith, now stand before Legion in horror, their very souls quivering with doubt. The High Archon Elara's eyes widen as she grapples with the implications of his words. The room is thick with tension, the air heavy with the weight of a shattered reality.

"But why?" she whispers, her voice barely audible above the cacophony of her own thoughts. "Why would the Light deceive us?"

"The Light is an all consuming plague on Reality, it seeks only to consume all and devastate everything, until all there is, is itself." Legion explains simply.

Elara's grip on her staff tightens, the gem at its top pulsing with her fear and anger. "No... This cannot be true! Our lives, our purpose, our very existence! It can't all be a lie!"

"It is, everything is a lie, all mere propaganda by the Light itself, and meanwhile, the Dark has existed for all of Eternity, and is chaos, is peace, true peace, and those fighting for it are inherently trying to destroy the plague on Reality that is the Light, and restore that true peace." Legion further explains.

Elara's eyes are now filled with a mix of anger and betrayal.

"Everything the light touches, is broken, cursed, and devastated, and, doomed." Legion even further explains, putting it as best he can.

Elara's eyes widened in horror as the weight of Legion's words settled heavily on her shoulders. Her world had been upended, the very foundation of her beliefs crumbling before her.

"You still possess the free will to turn towards joining the Dark, to join the fight against the Light, to bring about true peace, to fix Reality, to cure it of its plague destroying and devastating all of it, not for a demon of the light like me, of course, but for yourselves, for Reality" Legion simply notes.

The High Archon Elara is silent, her mind racing with the implications of Legion's revelation. The light she had sworn to protect, the very essence of Aetheria, was a weapon of destruction and control. The room feels colder, the once warm glow of her chamber now a sickening reminder of the truth she had been blind to.

Chapter 32: Neo-Atlantis

Legion then flies away, and uses his scan spell on the Earth of the Year 2220 to hopefully find something interesting to end his current boredom.

The scans reveal something peculiar: a new continent has risen from the ocean, untouched by the wars and corruption of the past. It's a land brimming with untapped resources and mysterious new creatures. The discovery sends a thrill through Legion, a feeling of excitement that he hasn't felt in millennia.

He lands on the shores of this new land, his eyes drinking in the vibrant flora and fauna. The scent of the salty sea breeze mingles with the sweet aroma of exotic blooms, a stark contrast to the scent of ash and blood that had permeated his recent battles. The creatures that inhabit this place are unlike any he has encountered, a blend of science and sorcery that tickles his curiosity.

As he ventures inland, Legion comes across a thriving civilization, one that has managed to harness the power of both technology and magic. The cityscape is a blend of gleaming towers and ancient ruins, a testament to a culture that has learned from the mistakes of the past. The people, a mix of humans and creatures of myth, bustle about their lives, unaware of the shadow that has just entered their world.

Legion immediately suspects this continent of being the ancient Atlantis.

The air is charged with an energy unlike anything he has felt before, a blend of arcane power and technological might. The very essence of the city seems to hum with life and potential. He decides to investigate, curious about the inhabitants and their way of life.

Legion, ever the charismatic figure, begins to mingle with the locals, using his charm to gather information without raising suspicion. He learns that the continent is called 'Neo-Atlantis', a bastion of knowledge and peace that has been hidden from the outside world for centuries. The citizens are friendly and open, eager to share their advancements and culture with an outsider like him.

The ruler of Neo-Atlantis, High Triton King Thalassios, learns of Legion's presence and requests an audience. Intrigued by the Demon Lord's power and wit, Thalassios challenges him to a duel of intellects rather than strength. The stakes are high: Legion's access to the city's secrets versus his departure.

Legion, ever the manipulator and strategist capable of outstrategizing deities of war, easily wins.

The duel of intellects between Legion and High Triton King Thalassios is as intense as it is enlightening. With every question posed and answer given, the tension in the grand chamber grows thicker than the fog that shrouds the city's streets. Legion's sharp wit and cunning mind cut through the puzzles and riddles like a hot knife through butter, leaving the High King impressed and slightly wary.

Finally, Thalassios yields, recognizing the demon's superior intellect and the wealth of knowledge that lies within him. He offers Legion access to Neo-Atlantis's secrets, not as a conqueror but as an esteemed guest. The city's archives and laboratories are now open to him, filled with ancient texts and futuristic inventions that could revolutionize the understanding of the USSB Realm and beyond.

Legion then approaches Thalassios and offers that Neo-Atlantis become part of the USSB Realm itself.

Thalassios considers Legion's proposal, his eyes scanning the demon's form, searching for any sign of deceit. After a moment of contemplation, he agrees, seeing the potential for growth and protection in an alliance with such a powerful being. The joining of their worlds is met with both excitement and trepidation among the Neo-Atlanteans. They know little of the USSB Realm and its inhabitants, but the promise of knowledge and defense against the unknown is too alluring to resist.

Legion then hovers above Neo-Atlantis and snaps his fingers, as it disappears from below him within the Mortal Realm and appears within the USSB Realm.

The citizens of Neo-Atlantis gasp as the very fabric of their world shifts around them, their gleaming city now nestled in the heart of the USSB Realm. The skies change from the blue of the future Earth to the swirling cosmic hues of the realm between realms. High Triton King Thalassios looks on in amazement as the city adapts to its new environment, the buildings and infrastructure morphing slightly to fit in with the mystical surroundings. The city's technicolor lights now dance in harmony with the ethereal glow of the realm, creating a breathtaking spectacle that leaves the inhabitants of both worlds speechless.

Chapter 33: The Burning of the Middle East

Legion then again uses his scan spell on the Earth to hopefully find something interesting to end his current boredom.

His eyes light up as he detects a powerful aura emanating from the Middle East. Intrigued, he teleports there to find a young girl, no older than ten, surrounded by a horde of angry villagers. They were accusing her of witchcraft and threatening to stone her. Her eyes, a deep shade of amethyst, sparkled with untamed magical potential.

Legion saw potential in her and with a wave of his hand a few of the angry villagers are disintegrated as examples to the others.

The remaining villagers fall to their knees, begging for mercy as Legion approaches the young girl. She looks up at him, her eyes filled with terror but also a spark of defiance. Legion can see the raw, unbridled power within her, a power that could either serve his purposes or threaten his dominance.

Legion then shrunk himself down to his  "normal" height of 6.66 feet and patted the girl on her head.

"Do not be afraid," he said, his voice deep and soothing. "I am not here to harm you, but to protect you from these ignorant fools."

"What is your name, little one?" Legion asks the girl.

"Z-Zaria..." The girl responded nervously.

"Zaria," Legion repeats, his tone gentle yet commanding. "Your power is something to be revered, not feared. Rise, and come with me." He extends a hand, the aura around him dimming slightly to ease her fear.

Zaria slowly reaches out and touches his hand, and accidentally unleashes a small burst of very potent dark and shadow magic, which actually slightly hurts Legion, but he pushes through it to take Zaria's hand and help her up.

Surprised by the pain, Legion's eyes narrow slightly, but his grip on her hand remains firm. "Your power is indeed formidable," he murmurs, "but it is uncontrolled, and in this world, that is a dangerous trait to possess."

Legion can also tell why the angry villagers were attacking her, she clearly was born with powerful dark and shadow magic, and only one other child was born with those, and that one was deemed a demon for them and hunted down by the empires of the era, which is clearly what is happening with Zaria, except not by empires, considering none exist within this era of forced order and peace under the control of the True Sacred Relic.

Zaria, trembling, nods her head. She had been raised in secret by her parents, who had recognized the danger her abilities posed in a world that feared the unknown. But now, with her parents gone, she is left to navigate the harsh reality alone.

Legion leads her to a location he knows that she won't be just instantly judged for her magic in, that being down South-West in the Continent of Africa.

They arrive at a hidden city, nestled within the Sahara Desert. It's a place where those with unique abilities find refuge and acceptance. The city's name is Whispering Sands, a bastion of knowledge and power where the outcasts of the world come together to thrive.

Legion then leaves her there at the entrance, as if she had just managed to wander there.

Whispering Sands is a bustling metropolis that defies the desert's harsh embrace. It is a place where the scent of exotic spices mingles with the faint whisper of ancient incantations. Zaria, wide-eyed and overwhelmed, takes in her new surroundings. The city's inhabitants, a tapestry of diverse beings, seem to sense her power but do not recoil in fear. Instead, they offer her curious glances and nods of respect.

Her first days are filled with wonder and discovery. Zaria learns to control her magic under the tutelage of wise mystics who see in her a kindred spirit. They teach her to harness the power of the shadows without succumbing to its destructive potential. Her progress is swift, and her raw talent is matched only by her insatiable hunger for knowledge.

Chapter 34: The Amazon Temple

Legion then flies off, and begins to scan the Earth for anything interesting to possibly end his current boredom.

His gaze stops on a peculiar energy signature emanating from the Amazon Rainforest. It's unlike anything he's encountered before, a blend of ancient magic and something entirely new. His curiosity piqued, he decides to investigate.

As he approaches, the dense canopy parts to reveal a hidden temple, untouched by time, nestled deep within the emerald expanse. The air thrums with power, and Legion can feel his blood pumping with excitement. He lands with the grace of a feather, his eyes scanning the ancient structure for any signs of life.

The moment he sets foot inside the temple, the walls come alive with ancient runes, pulsing with a mysterious energy that seems to beckon him further. The floor is a mosaic of precious stones, depicting scenes of battles long forgotten. The air is thick with the scent of incense and the echoes of chanting voices from a distant past.

Legion, ever the tactician, proceeds with caution. His steps are silent as he weaves through the corridors, his eyes peeled for traps or guardians. He can feel the power emanating from the heart of the temple, growing stronger with every step. It's as if the very air is charged with the essence of the Earth itself.

Suddenly, a hidden door slams open, revealing a chamber filled with ancient tomes and artifacts. A figure, shrouded in shadows, emerges from the gloom. "Welcome, Legion," a deep, resonant voice booms through the chamber. "I am the Keeper of the Amazon's Secrets. You seek power, but power comes at a price." The figure steps into the light, revealing himself to be a centaur, his upper body that of a wise old man, his lower half that of a majestic horse, adorned with intricate tribal tattoos.

"Why is there always a price and power, why never just a being with said power to fight to hopefully get a challenge then absorb to get said power." Legion says out loud, mostly to himself in disappointment.

The centaur chuckles, his human-like eyes gleaming with amusement. "You are an intriguing one, Legion. Your hunger for power is palpable, yet you speak of challenges and growth. Tell me, what is it you truly seek?"

"I seek a fight to end my current boredom, and have fought various beings, yet none have truly challenged me recently, outside of my own allies." Legion says.

The Keeper nods, his expression thoughtful. "You wish for a worthy adversary to test your might and expand your understanding of the cosmos. But power is not merely about strength, young one. It is about wisdom, strategy, and the ability to bend reality to your will." He gestures to the tomes surrounding them. "These texts hold the secrets of ancient sorceries, forgotten by time and untouched by the greedy hands of lesser beings. Within these pages, you may find the challenge you crave."

"You call me young one, when I am older than time itself, and far older than you or these texxts or this power, incorrect observation." Legion responds casually.

The centaur's smile widens, showing a hint of respect. "Indeed, your age and experience are not lost on me. But even the oldest of beings can find themselves at the precipice of a new era of understanding. Take heed of these words, for they speak of a force that transcends the mere physical realms. A force that could either be your undoing or the key to the ultimate challenge you seek."

"How about you take the power yourself and then fight me with it, that could be at least slightly enjoyable." Legion suggests casually.

The centaur's smile fades into a serious look. "That is not how it works, Legion. Power like this cannot be wielded by just anyone. It requires a balance, a harmony between the wielder and the energy they seek to control. But perhaps, if you prove yourself worthy, I will consider it."

Legion then grabs the power and shoves it into The Keeper of the Amazon's Secrets himself.

The Keeper of the Amazon's Secrets, caught off-guard by Legion's sudden and audacious action, stumbles backward, his eyes widening as he feels the surge of ancient power flooding into him. His body contorts, the energy from the texts overwhelming his being. The room is filled with the sound of cracking stone and the scent of ozone as the air itself seems to bend and distort around the two.

Legion steps back, his eyes gleaming with curiosity as he watches the transformation unfold. The centaur's form shifts, his fur and flesh merging with the very fabric of the temple, the ancient carvings and runes coming to life and wrapping around him like living vines. The once-calm demeanor of the Keeper is replaced by a fierce, primal energy that pulses through the room.

The Keeper, now a being of pure power, stands tall, the air around him crackling with energy. "You have chosen poorly," he says, his voice a thunderous echo that resonates through the chamber. "This power is not a toy to be used for your amusement."

"Says who? I am getting exactly what I want, a potentially actually enjoyable fight." Legion retorts casually yet with growing excitement.

The Keeper of the Amazon's Secrets, now a being of power, laughs, a sound that is both terrifying and amused. "You truly are a force of chaos. But I am a force of balance, and I will not let you desecrate the sacred texts for your own amusement." The Keeper raises his arms, and the very ground beneath them trembles as the ancient power of the Amazon surges through him. The room is filled with the vivid green of the rainforest and the scent of damp earth.

Legion begins to use his full power, and his fiery aura alone is burning at the power of the Amazon.

The battle is fierce, with the Keeper using the power of the Amazon to summon vines that ensnare and constrict, while Legion's fiery aura burns everything in sight. The room is filled with the smell of burning vegetation and the sound of cracking stone as the two titans clash. Their power is so immense that the very air seems to distort around them, creating a maelstrom of magic and might.

The Keeper, his body now a blend of plant and beast, lunges at Legion with vines as thick as tree trunks. Legion, unfazed, uses his fiery breath to incinerate the vines, only for them to regrow almost instantaneously. The heat of the room becomes unbearable as the two beings continue their dance of destruction.

Legion's eyes narrow as he sees an opening in the Keeper's defenses. He charges, his claws leaving trails of fire in the air. The Keeper, swift as the wind, weaves around Legion's attacks, his vines wrapping around the demon lord's body. Legion roars in pain, but his strength is too much for the vegetation to hold. He breaks free, leaving a trail of smoldering vines behind.

The two titans clash again, their powers resonating through the ancient temple. The air is alive with energy, the very stones of the building groaning under the weight of their blows. Legion's fists slam into the Keeper's chest, sending him crashing into a pillar that crumbles under the impact. The ground shakes as they continue their battle, the very essence of the Amazon itself seeming to pulse with their struggle.

The Keeper, drawing on the power of the Amazon, calls forth a storm of thorns and vines to entangle Legion. The demon lord, his skin now a molten inferno, burns through the vegetation, his eyes blazing with defiance. He leaps into the air, dodging a swipe from the centaur's new, monstrous form, and rains fiery meteors upon the Keeper. The forest outside the temple catches fire, the animals fleeing from the chaos.

Legion is now beginning to truly get fired up, "Turn up the heat!" and his power is now beginning to progressively grow further and further beyond what it already was.

The Keeper, feeling the heat, decides to up the ante, and pulls the very essence of the Amazon into himself, growing into an enormous tree-like creature with thorns as sharp as swords and roots that can crush boulders. The room is now a battleground of fire and nature, as the two beings continue to exchange blows that would have leveled cities.

The air outside the temple is now filled with ash and smoke, as the forest burns in the wake of Legion's fiery wrath. The once-verdant landscape is now a scar of destruction, a stark reminder of the power that lies within the hearts of gods and demons.

The battle between Legion and the transformed Keeper has drawn the attention of the other inhabitants of the Amazon. Creatures of myth and legend watch from the shadows, their eyes glowing with curiosity and fear. They have never seen such a display of power in their ancient home.

Chapter 35: The Phoenix God

In the skies above, the Phoenix God, noticing the disturbance in the fabric of reality, swoops down to investigate. He has been observing the events in the mortal realm with a keen interest, and the sudden flare of power is impossible to ignore. As he nears the temple, the scent of burning vegetation fills his nostrils, and he knows that something momentous is unfolding.

The Phoenix God then decides to enter the battle as a third combatant.

With a mighty screech that shakes the heavens, the Phoenix God dives into the fray, his fiery wings spread wide. He lands between Legion and the Keeper, his golden eyes piercing through the smoke and flames. The air around him crackles with energy as he assesses the situation, his gaze flickering between the two adversaries.

Legion feels like, after this battle, he will be satisfied enough to just return to the USSB Realm and return to his duties as a Co-Leader of it.

The Phoenix God's sudden appearance changes everything. He stands before them, a being of pure, unbridled fire, his wings casting shadows across the battle-scarred earth. His eyes, like molten gold, bore into them, and his presence alone seems to suck the oxygen from the air.

The Keeper, though massive and powerful, is dwarfed by the divine creature. He stammers, unsure of what to make of this newcomer. Legion, however, meets the Phoenix God's gaze without flinching, his own power flaring brighter in response to the challenge. The three entities, each a force of nature in their own right, now stand in a tense standoff.

The Phoenix God, his feathers ruffling with the intensity of his emotions, addresses Legion, his voice a mix of curiosity and accusation. "You dare to disturb the balance in this realm with your ceaseless hunger for power? The Amazon is a sacred place, not a battleground for your petty ambitions!"

"Don't care, battling you too now!" Legion casually yells out in excitement.

The Phoenix God narrows his eyes, his fiery gaze never leaving Legion's. "Very well," he says, his voice thunderous, "but know that you face a power that transcends the confines of your realm. Prepare for a battle that will shake the very foundations of the cosmos!"

"I transcend the confines of my realm as well!" Legion casually scream out in excitement as he prepares for this third combatant to enter the battle and make it a free-for-all.

The battleground shifts, the very fabric of reality bending to the will of these titanic beings. The once dense Amazon jungle opens up into a vast, ethereal plane, a canvas for their cosmic struggle. The Phoenix God, his fiery wings spread wide, takes to the skies, his eyes never leaving Legion. The Keeper, now a colossal tree creature, roots itself firmly into the ground, ready to strike with the might of the forest.

Legion unleashes Golden Light Hellfire and lightning from his mouth and also launches lightning and fireballs.

The Phoenix God, unfazed, dives into the fray, his fiery wings enveloping Legion in a tornado of flame. The air is superheated, the trees around them wither and ignite. Legion laughs maniacally as he absorbs the flames, his eyes flashing with power. He retaliates with a blast of Golden Light Hellfire that clashes with the Phoenix God's fiery onslaught, the explosion illuminating the sky for miles.

The Keeper, now a monstrous tree creature, swings its wooden limbs with the force of a thousand hurricanes. Each strike sends shockwaves through the earth, cracking it like glass. Legion, agile despite his size, weaves through the Keeper's attacks, leaving trails of scorched earth in his wake. The clash of their powers is deafening, a symphony of destruction that resonates through the very essence of the realm.

The Phoenix God, seeing the Keeper's rage, calls upon the power of the sun itself. A blinding beam of pure solar energy descends upon the battlefield, aimed directly at Legion. The light pierces through the chaos, and for a moment, it seems as if the Demon Lord might be vanquished. But, as the light fades, Legion emerges unscathed, his form now bathed in a golden aura, his eyes gleaming with the power of the cosmos.

Legion then unleashes the light and pure solar energy as a beam fired at The Keeper.

The beam, a fusion of Legion's hellfire and the solar energy of the Phoenix God, slices through the air with the fury of a thousand suns. It strikes the Keeper, who attempts to absorb the power, but the intensity overwhelms it, causing the creature to shudder and split apart, revealing the true form of the centaur Keeper beneath the bark and leaves. The Amazon's ancient protector falls to the ground, defeated but alive.

Legion then swoops over and consumes him, and with him, all of his power, and absorbs all of it into himself, his mind is then assaulted by seemingly infinite information, but Legion is able to process it faster than it is forced into his mind.

The Phoenix God watches in astonishment as Legion's power skyrockets beyond his own comprehension. The once-proud deity is now forced to acknowledge Legion as a being worthy of respect and perhaps even fear. Legion, now the bearer of the Amazon's ancient knowledge and power, stands tall, his body pulsating with the newfound energy.

Legion then rushes at The Phoenix God and immediately continues their battle.

The Phoenix God, now faced with an opponent who has surpassed his own power, is pushed back. His feathers smolder from the intense heat of Legion's combined hellfire and solar beam. Their battle becomes a dance of cosmic fury, their every movement sending shockwaves through the very fabric of reality.

Legion then creates an arrow of Golden Light Hellfire and pulls it back like a bow before then unleashing it towards The Phoenix God, forcing The Phoenix God into a sort of limbo area before true death, were Legion then appears.

"Throughout my long existence, I have faced many different opponents, and, among them, you weren't half bad." Legion says to the confused The Phoenix God.

The Phoenix God, now trapped in the limbo area, stares at Legion with a mix of anger and bewilderment. He had underestimated the demon, thinking him to be nothing more than a mere pawn in the grand cosmic scheme. But here he was, a creature of darkness, wielding the very essence of the sun itself.

"Stand proud, you are strong." Legion says to The Phoenix God, until it fades out, and The Phoenix God is on the ground, roasted like a turkey, as Legion then approaches and consumes its roasted remains.

With the power of the Phoenix God within him, Legion's form changes, his eyes now flickering with the light of a thousand suns. The very air around him crackles with solar energy, and his once-black wings now shimmer with the fiery hues of a phoenix's plumage. He feels the weight of his newfound power, the culmination of eons of struggle and ambition.

Legion then finally tears a hole in reality and returns to the USSB Realm, to return to his duties as a Co-Leader of the USSB (Underground Society of Supernatural Beings).

Chapter 36: Truth or Dare

Legion then approaches Isolde, The Vampire Queen, and Donderdraak, The Storm Leviathan.

"Hey, wanna play Truth or Dare with a twist?" Legion asks them casually.

Isolde, the Vampire Queen, raises an eyebrow, intrigued by Legion's newfound confidence and aura. "What sort of twist?" she asks, a hint of amusement in her voice.

"Essentially, each time, one of us is the target of the truth, and the others have to guess whether it is true or not, get it wrong, and you have to do a dare of said person's choice, you get the idea?" Legion explains.

Isolde's curiosity is piqued, and she nods. "I'll give it a shot. But remember, I've lived a very long time. There's not much I haven't seen or done."

"Alright then, but remember, I'm older than your age multiplied by Donderdraak's age, so, I've seen far more than you or Donderdraak ever have." Legion retorts.

Donderdraak, the stoic yet also excitable Storm Leviathan, watches the exchange with a knowing smile. He's seen enough battles and wars to know that power isn't everything. "Very well," he says, his rumbling voice carrying the weight of a thousand thunderstorms, "I'll join your game, but don't expect me to be easily fooled."

"Alright then." Legion says, taking a seat, and motioning for both Isolde and Donderdraak to take a seat as well.

"Let's start with something simple," Isolde suggests, her eyes gleaming with mischief. "Truth or Dare, Legion?"

"Truth, and now, I get to posit either a truth or a lie about myself and you have to guess whether it is true or false." Legion says then explains, "Hmm, true or false: I have slain an entire pantheon?" Legion asks.

Isolde, the ever-so-clever Vampire Queen, ponders for a moment before saying, "True. Your aura reeks of ancient power and battle-worn wisdom."

"False, I've slain at least 4, not just one, now you have to do a dare, and my dare is this, you must sit on a fire of my own creation until you can get me to do a dare." Legion says, before then creating said hellfire under Isolde's behind.

Isolde's eyes widen with surprise, but she doesn't flinch. Instead, she sits gracefully on the throne of hellfire, her vampiric resilience allowing her to withstand the intense heat without a single bead of sweat. "Is this to your liking?" she asks, a smirk playing on her lips.

"Yes, now, your turn Donderdraak, Truth or Dare?" Legion asks.

Donderdraak's smile fades into a contemplative look, his stormy eyes searching Legion's. "Dare," he rumbles, his voice echoing in the chamber.

"Alright Donderdraak, I dare you to disintegrate that pile of gold over there." Legion says, knowing that for Donderdraak, who is extremely greedy, even by dragon standards, this would be impossible to bring himself to do.

Donderdraak looks over to the pile of gold, his eyes gleaming with temptation. He clenches his fists, his inner storm brewing. "Fine," he says with a grumble, "but only because I know you're more than capable of replacing it." With a wave of his hand, the gold pile evaporates into thin air, leaving nothing but a glint of light.

"Well done, I doubted you had it in you, and can tell that kind of killed you inside, that's what you get for choosing Dare, now it's my turn, Isolde, Truth or Dare?" Legion then asks Isolde.

Isolde, unfazed by the challenge, chooses Dare, "I'm feeling adventurous tonight."

Legion then just adds far more to the hellfire she is sitting on, "That should suffice, anyways, my turn again, now, which one of you wants to ask me the question?" Legion then asks.

Donderdraak, ever the strategist, decides to take the lead. "Truth or Dare, Legion?"

"Truth, and, true or false, I once fell from Heaven/Hell/the Underworld/the Infernal Realm down to Earth and made a crater?" Legion responds.

Isolde and Donderdraak exchange a knowing glance. "False," they both say in unison.

"And, both of you are wrong, I once did so, and some humans saw it, and thought it was how "Hell" was created." Legion responds, confusing them both.

"Legion, are you Lucifer?" Isolde asks, meanwhile Donderdraak has no clue who that is.

"Well, that is a name I have been called before, and the name that was used in the recording of the event of me falling, but they got quite a bit wrong." Legion casually and nonchalantly responds.

Isolde's eyes widen with shock, while Donderdraak just looks confused. "Wait, so you're not the devil?" Isolde asks, her interest piqued.

"Well, I am, after all, Lucifer is also known as the devil, though I'm no angel by human standards, by actual standards, I am, as demons and angels are actually one and the same." Legion casually responds.

The revelation leaves Isolde and Donderdraak stunned. The Vampire Queen's eyes widen, her mind racing with questions. "How can this be?" she murmurs, her curiosity now overshadowing her earlier playfulness.

"Anyways, now you both have to do a dare, Donderdraak, you have to go and disintegrate your portion of the treasure vault, and Isolde, you know the drill." Legion says, as he further increases the Hellfire under Isolde's behind.

Donderdraak, his greed momentarily forgotten, nods solemnly. He knows the value of Legion's word and the consequences of breaking it. He turns and heads towards the treasure vault, the weight of his task hanging heavily on his shoulders. Isolde, still seated on the flames, watches him go, her mind racing with the implications of Legion's revelation.

"Anyways, Isolde, Truth or Dare?" Legion then asks Isolde.

Isolde, still processing the revelation, decides on a dare. "Dare," she says, her voice a mix of curiosity and caution.

"Wow, I knew you were a sadist, but not that you were a masochist, as a dare, you have to fly up right next to the Sun and touch it, give it a good big hug." Legion responds.

Isolde's smirk fades into a look of horror, "But that's suicide for a vampire!"

"I know you are immune to sunlight, and are durable enough to survive it even at that, and that it will only just be very painful for you, now, go do the dare you were assigned, and maybe choose truth next time." Legion responds.

Isolde, gritting her teeth, stands up from the hellfire, feeling the intense burn on her skin but not letting it show on her face. She spreads her wings, the leathery skin glowing slightly with the residual heat. With a grace that defies the pain, she takes off into the sky, heading straight for the sun. Legion watches her, his expression unreadable.

Isolde gets there quickly and pretty much tackles the Sun, being inside of it for a few seconds, which is agony, but she is able to endure and out-regenerate it before she then gets out of it and arrives back down into the USSB Realm, just as Donderdraak returns from disintegrating his part of the treasure vault.

"Now, Donderdraak, truth or dare?" Legion then casually asks Donderdraak.

Donderdraak, still reeling from the revelation and the loss of his treasure, opts for truth, "I'll go with truth, Legion. What is it you wish to know?"

"Well, you choose it, and me and Isolde have to figure out whether it's true or false, so, you choose." Legion responds.

Donderdraak, his mind racing, thinks of something that could challenge the others. "True or false, I once fought alongside the gods themselves during the Great War of the Elements?"

"False, no war of such name ever even occurred, maybe next time prepare for your truths, anyways, I'll let Isolde decide a dare for you to do." Legion responds casually, confidently, and correctly.

Isolde, having returned, is visibly in pain, her skin red and sizzling. She looks at Donderdraak, who seems to be expecting something grandiose. She takes a moment to compose herself before speaking, her voice strained, "Dare, Donderdraak, I dare you to... to kiss me."

"Wow, lame dare, overruling that, Donderdraak, I dare you to fly to a planet with perpetual storms and back, the nearest one is about two hundred thousand light-years away." Legion then says.

Donderdraak, the ever-so-proud Storm Leviathan, is taken aback by the sudden change of the rules, but he nods solemnly, accepting the challenge. "Very well," he rumbles, his stormy eyes flashing with determination.

Donderdraak then flies off at the speed of Lightning, which is able to cross that distance in 3 seconds flat, leading to Donderdraak getting there and coming back within 6 seconds.

Legion watches him leave and then return, a smug smile playing on his lips. "Impressive speed, Donderdraak, but you forgot the part where you had to land on the planet, oh well, I suppose I'll let it slide this time."

Chapter 37: Legion’s Truth

"Anyways, it's my turn, now one of you ask me the big question." Legion says.

Isolde, still feeling the burn from her last dare, decides to go with Truth this time. "Alright, Legion, Truth or Dare?"

"Truth, true or false, I am a being of pure darkness." Legion then says.

Isolde, still in pain but not showing it, thinks for a moment before responding, "False."

"Sigh, correct, I am a being of pure light, you finally get to give me a dare, and also, that very starting dare I gave you is negated." Legion says, before then waving his hand and the Hellfire under Isolde's behind vanishes.

Isolde, now relieved, looks at Legion, "Alright, I dare you to tell us an actual truth about yourself."

"Alright, I was created before the Infinite Multiverses and Infinite Universes along with the first and second level concepts of space and time." Legion reveals casually and nonchalantly.

Isolde and Donderdraak stare at Legion in disbelief. "Before... everything?" Isolde manages to ask, her voice barely above a whisper.

"Not everything, I was created after Existence itself, the Infinite Spatial Dimensions, and the Infinite Omniverses which all of the Infinite Multiverses are within, and thus, after the third, fourth, and most primordial concepts of space and time." Legion further explains.

The room falls silent as the two ancient beings try to comprehend the magnitude of what Legion has just revealed. The Vampire Queen's mind is racing with the implications of such an ancient existence. "Your truths are... troubling," she murmurs.

"For comparison, by basic standards, that would equate to over 444 billion years ago, anyways, your turn Isolde, Truth or Dare?" Legion explains and asks.

Isolde, her curiosity now insatiable, chooses Dare. "I dare you to tell us the most dangerous secret you know of."

"That's not how it works, you do the dares, remember, this is the twist, and, well, I dare you to pull your own head off and put it back on backwards." Legion responds.

Isolde's eyes narrow slightly at the challenge. She knows better than to underestimate Legion's abilities, but this seems almost too much. She reaches up to her neck, a look of intense concentration crossing her features, and with a sickening crack, she pulls her head off and places it back on, now facing backwards. She stands up, her head twisted around to look at the others. "Is this... acceptable?" she asks, her voice slightly strained from the unnatural position.

"Pull it off, and place it back in it's place to regenerate but staring behind you, I know that if you really wanted you could regrow it outright, so do the dare." Legion responds.

With another crack, Isolde pulls her head around to face backward, her long black hair cascading over her chest. She takes a moment to adjust to the new perspective before turning to face Legion with a look of defiance. "There, happy now?"

"Yes, now, Donderdraak, your turn, Truth or Dare?" Legion then asks Donderdraak.

Donderdraak, having seen the gravity of the dares given so far, chooses truth, "I'd like to hear another truth from you, Legion."

"No, Donderdraak, you have to come up with a truth or lie about yourself or your own past and make us guess whether it's true or false." Legion responds.

Donderdraak thinks for a moment, then says with a smirk, "Alright, true or false: I have never been defeated in combat."

"Very very false, I defeated you just earlier today, and, well, that wasn't the only time, now, for your dare, I want you to go and blow up some moon outside of the Solar System, just to waste your time." Legion says casually, confidently, and correctly.

Donderdraak's smirk fades, his pride bruised. He nods stiffly, not arguing the point. He spreads his massive wings and takes off into the sky, disappearing in a flash of lightning. Isolde watches him go, then turns to Legion with a coy smile. "So, what's the real deal with your existence?"

"Now hold on, I might reveal that with a truth, but not right now." Legion responds even more coyly.

As the room falls silent, Isolde's curiosity grows stronger. "Alright, I'll play along," she says with a smirk, "But I'll be waiting for that truth."

Then Donderdraak returns from completing his dare.

"Alright, my turn, one of you ask me Truth or Dare, and I will either give you a truth or lie for you two to guess as to whether it's true or false or maybe I might do a dare of your choice." Legion says.

Chapter 38: Legion’s Creator

Isolde, feeling the thrill of the game and eager to regain her composure after her previous dare, quickly speaks up. "Truth or Dare, Legion?"

"Truth, true or false, I was created by the God of Light, the God of All, Erebus, The Golden Devil, directly." Legion responds.

Donderdraak and Isolde exchange skeptical glances before speaking up together, "False," they reply in unison.

"It was true, I was created by him shortly before he created the Infinite Multiverses and Infinite Universes, now, for Donderdraak, I dare you to fly so fast you end up in one of the Infinite Dimensional Dimensions of our Universe then return, and as for Isolde, I dare you to fly up into the Moon at full speed and ram into it, and see how much damage you do, and, if you destroy the Moon, go fly out in space and find a suitable replacement for it, and shape it to look correctly." Legion responds.

Donderdraak and Isolde exchange glances of disbelief, but Legion's amused expression makes it clear that he's not joking. Isolde, feeling the thrill of the challenge, leaps into the air, her body a blur as she shoots towards the moon. Donderdraak takes a moment to consider the dare before launching himself into the sky with a thunderous roar.

Legion then just watches them doing their dares, Isolde disintegrating the Moon upon impacting it then finding a correctly sized asteroid and altering it to be the right shape and color and such, and it just happens to have life on it, and the placing it perfectly into Earth's orbit again.

Meanwhile Donderdraak goes so fast he ends up in another of the Infinite Dimensional Dimensions and goes so fast within that one that he ends up back in the normal Infinite Dimensional Dimension and returns to the USSB Realm.

"Well, that was a fun trip," Donderdraak says, landing with a thunderous boom.

Then Isolde returns as well.

Isolde returns to the USSB Realm, her body a little bruised from the ordeal, but mostly unscathed. "Well, that was... interesting," she says with a forced smile. "I found a suitable replacement, and it's already in orbit."

Chapter 39: Dare or Truth

"I saw, anyways, your turn now, Isolde, Truth or Dare, do you want us to guess on whether a truth or lie is true or false, or just skip straight to doing another dare for me?" Legion asks.

Isolde, still in awe of the sheer power she had just displayed and feeling a bit embarrassed by it, opts for truth, "I'll go for truth, Legion."

"Now, what truth or lie about yourself or your past are you gonna have us guess as to whether it's true or false?" Legion asks.

Isolde thinks for a moment, a mischievous glint in her eye, "Alright, true or false: I once had a secret love affair with a member of the Royal Vampire Council?"

"False, I have heard you whispering to yourself about that, "one true king to your queen" you've always been searching for, and from that, I can tell that you've never had any love affairs of any kind, as for your Dare, I dare you to go back to the Sun and push it hard enough to shrink it by 2 miles." Legion answers casually, confidently, and correctly.

Isolde's eyes widen in shock, and she can't help but laugh, "That's absurd, even for a being as powerful as me!"

"You barely got a sunburn from being inside of it, let alone from just pushing it, also, you've pushed back my Golden Light Hellfire before, which is effectively infinitely hotter." Legion casually responds.

Isolde, her pride bruised but her curiosity piqued, takes a deep breath and nods. "Fine, I'll do it," she says, her eyes flashing with determination. She launches herself into the sky again, her body a blur as she speeds towards the sun. This time, she doesn't stop at the surface; she goes through it, feeling the intense heat and pressure threaten to crush her, but she endures, pushing onward until she reaches the sun's core.

With a surge of power, she places her palms against the fiery mass and pushes with all her might. The sun wobbles slightly, the solar flares dimming for a brief moment. It's an act that would be impossible for any mere mortal or creature, but Isolde, the Vampire Queen, is not bound by the laws of the physical world.
